    Once again, we note the fact that \( y \) and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables. Thus, \eqref{eq:a-modified-z-e0-modified-y-ay} applies
    and \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay-modified-z-mapsto-e0} implies \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-a-modified-x-mapsto-ay} and subsequently
    \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-z-mapsto-e0} by the induction hypothesis.
    Since \( e_{0} \) is arbitrary, it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true, thus
    establishing the converse of \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Finally, we consider the case where \( \varphi \) is an existentially quantified formula; i.e., \( \varphi \coloneq \exists z \, \psi \) for some \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula
    \( \psi \) and \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable \( z \) such that \( z \neq x \) and \( z \neq y \).
    Clearly, every free occurrence of \( x \) in \( \varphi \) is also a free occurence in \( \psi \).
    Therefore, \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \exists z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \).
    Again, we must show that \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}, \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication} and their respective converses are true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \). Thus, there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-for-some-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \psi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
        \ \ \text{for some} \  e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), applying the induction hypothesis to \eqref{eq:psi-is-satisfied-by-modified-a-for-some-e0} once again
    yields \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}.
    However,
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) }
     = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    due to \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    and the fact that \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables.
    From \eqref{eq:psi-x-mapsto-y-is-satisfied-using-e0}, we can now infer
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} }      
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \coloneq \exists z \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} \), we conclude by
    \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }
    \end{equation*}
    This establishes \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication}.
    The converse of \eqref{eq:first-statement-forward-implication} can be proven using a similar reasoning, only in reverse.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} } \)
    is true; that is, there exists some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables, we have by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{\mathfrak{a}(x)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(x) } }
    \end{equation*}
    By the induction hypothesis, we then have
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    The truth of \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is merely a consequence of
    \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove \eqref{eq:second-statement-forward-implication}, suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    is true. Thus, there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( \psi \) is a subformula of \( \varphi \), we conclude from the induction hypothesis that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } }
    \end{equation*}
    But \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) = \mathfrak{a}(y) \) because \( z \neq y \).
    Additionally, the fact that \( x \neq z \) means that \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} is applicable and we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } =
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    In other words, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }  \) is true and thus \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) as required.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove the converse, we reason like so.
    Suppose that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} } \) is true; that is,
    there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \(
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }
    \) is true.
    Since \( x \), \( y \), and \( z \) are distinct, it follows from
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } =
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}}
    \end{equation*}
    Thus, \(\folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{\mathfrak{a}(y)} }{z}{e_{0}} }\) implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }{x}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}}(y) } } \), which in turn implies
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\psi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{z}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    by the induction hypothesis.
    The truth of \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \mathfrak{a} } \) follows readily from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    It has been shown that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{ \mathfrak{a}(x) } }\)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    iff \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{ \mathfrak{a}(y)} } \)
    hold for any \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula \( \varphi \) with \( \folcomplexityofformula{\varphi} = n \) as long as the induction hypothesis is true.
    In other words, for any arbitrary \( n \in \naturalset \), the induction hypothesis implies \( P(n) \).
    The theorem follows readily from strong induction.
\end{proof}

\newpage
\section{Applications}

\noindent
We now have enough theoretical foundation to start proving many of the familiar results from logic.
\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:the-order-of-quantifiers-of-the-same-type-does-not-matter}
    Let \( \varphi \) denote an \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula. Let \( x \) and \( y \) denote \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables.
    Then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \forall x \, \forall y \, \varphi \equiv \forall y \, \forall x \, \varphi \) \phantomsection \label{enum:swapping-universal-quantifiers}
        \item \( \exists x \, \exists y \, \varphi \equiv \exists y \, \exists x \, \varphi \) \phantomsection \label{enum:swapping-existential-quantifiers}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \psi \coloneq \forall y \, \varphi \) and \( \phi \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \).
    To prove \ref{enum:swapping-universal-quantifiers}, we must demonstrate that \( \forall x \, \psi \equiv \forall y \, \phi \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \)
    under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-x-psi-is-satisfied-with-a}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:forall-x-psi-is-satisfied-with-a} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. Since
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \)
    and \( \psi \coloneq \forall y \, \varphi \),
    it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \).
    We now consider the only two possibilities that can arise under these circumstances: either \( x \) and \( y \) are the same \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable (\( x = y \))
    or they are distinct (\( x \neq y \)).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( x = y \), then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} \) by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function}.
    If \( x \neq y \),
    then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}}
    = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{1}} }{x}{e_{0}} \) by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    For either case, it is clear that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \)
    implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    Given that \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary,
    it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright) }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} } \);
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} } \). But \( e_{0} \) is also arbitrary,
    so necessarily \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall y \, \phi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    In other words,
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall x \, \psi \models \forall y \, \phi
    \end{equation*}
    For the converse, let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \) be any variable assignment function
    on \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall y \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Thus,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-y-phi-is-satisfied-with-b}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) be arbitrary. Therefore, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} } \)
    by \eqref{eq:forall-y-phi-is-satisfied-with-b}.
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) be another arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} } \) and
    \( \phi \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \), it follows from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    Again, we must consider two separate cases: \( x = y \) or \( x \neq y \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    If \( x = y \), then \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} \)
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function}.
    If \( x \neq y \), then
    \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} =
    \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} \) is also true
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    Clearly,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \)
    implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \)
    for either case.
    Since \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) is arbitrary, we infer from \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    But \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) is also arbitrary, so we conclude
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall y \, \phi \models \forall x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \forall x \, \psi \models \forall y \, \phi \) and \( \forall y \, \phi \models \forall x \, \psi \) are both true,
    it follows that \( \forall x \, \psi \equiv \forall y \, \phi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Part \ref{enum:swapping-existential-quantifiers} is a lot easier to prove. Again, we begin by redefining our notation.
    Let \( \psi \coloneq \exists y \, \varphi \) and \( \phi \coloneq \exists x \, \varphi \).
    The goal, therefore, is to show that \( \exists x \, \psi \equiv \exists y \, \phi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( \mathfrak{C} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{c} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \)
    under \( \mathfrak{C} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{c}} \);
    that is, there exists some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{C}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{0}}}\).
    Since \( \psi \coloneq \exists y \, \varphi \), it follows that there exists some \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{C}} \) such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\varphi}{\folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{0}}}{y}{e_{1}}}
    \end{equation*}
    If \( x \) and \( y \) are the same \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable, then
    \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}}
    = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \mathfrak{c} }{y}{e_{1}}
    = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} \)
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function}.
    If \( x \) and \( y \) are distinct, we derive the same conclusion by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    Clearly, for both cases, there exists an \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{C}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e} } \);
    that is, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{y}{e_{0}} } \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\mleft( \exists y \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{c}} \)
    is true. Since \( \mathfrak{C} \) and \( \mathfrak{c} \) are arbitrary, we conclude that
    \begin{equation*}
        \exists x \, \psi \models \exists y \, \phi
    \end{equation*}
    Conversely, let \( \mathfrak{D} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{d} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{D} \)
    such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{ \mleft( \exists y \, \phi \mright) }{\mathfrak{d}} \); that is, there exists an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{D}} \) such
    that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{y}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( \phi \coloneq \exists x \, \varphi \), there must exist some \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{D}} \)
    such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    Once again, we can apply \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-last-modification-will-take-effect-if-the-same-variable-symbol-is-modified-for-a-variable-assignment-function,thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} to conclude that
    such that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{1}}
        = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}}
    \end{equation*}
    in all possible cases. Thus, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{1}} } \) follows; i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{d}}{x}{e_{0}} } \), and thus
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{D}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{d}} \).
    Since \( \mathfrak{D} \) and \( \mathfrak{d} \) are both arbitrary, we conclude that
    \begin{equation*}
        \exists y \, \phi \models \exists x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
    Given that \( \exists x \, \psi \models \exists y \, \phi \) and \( \exists y \, \phi \models \exists x \, \psi \) are both true, we conclude
    that \( \exists x \, \psi \equiv \exists y \, \phi \) as needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:quantification-of-a-formula-with-a-non-free-variable-is-equivalent-to-the-original-formula}
    If \( \varphi \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable such that \( x \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \),
    then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \varphi \equiv \forall x \, \varphi \)
        \item \( \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \varphi \)
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary.
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{ \mathfrak{a} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} } =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds, it follows from
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) also holds.
    From this we arrive at two conclusions:
    \begin{enumerate*}[label=(\roman*)]
        \item since \( e_{0} \) denotes an arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \), we conclude that
            \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} } \) is true for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \);
        \item since \( \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \neq \emptyset \), we conclude that there exists some \( e_{0} \) such that
            \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    \end{enumerate*}
    In other words, \( \varphi \models \forall x \, \varphi \) and \( \varphi \models \exists x \, \varphi \) are both true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    The converse can be proven in a similar manner.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \) holds.
    Recall that mathematical structures must have non-empty universes. Therefore, it follows that \( \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \neq \emptyset \) and that
    there exists an \( e_{1} \) such that \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    Furthermore, since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \) holds,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) must be true
    by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.
    But \( x \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), so necessarily
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{ \mathfrak{b} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} } =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) holds,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{b}} \) is necessarily true by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}.
    Therefore, \( \forall x \, \varphi \models \varphi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( \mathfrak{C} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{c} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{C} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{c}} \); i.e., there exists an \( e_{2} \in \abs{\mathfrak{C}} \)
    such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{2}} } \) holds.
    Again, since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{ \mathfrak{c} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} } =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{2}} }{ \folfreevars{\varphi} }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{c}}{x}{e_{2}} } \) holds,
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    guarantees that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{C}}{\varphi}{\mathfrak{c}} \) is also true.
    Thus, \( \exists x \, \varphi \models \varphi \).
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:universal-quantifiers-distributes-over-conjunction}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is any \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable,
    then
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \equiv \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Thus, we have by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-x-phi-and-psi-is-satisfied}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} } \ \text{and}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} } \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary.
    Clearly, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds by
    \eqref{eq:forall-x-phi-and-psi-is-satisfied}.
    Since \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary,
    it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} } \) holds for
    all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    The same reasoning can be applied to show that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) also holds.
    In other words, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) follows from
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    for any arbitrary \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \).
    Therefore, \( \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \models \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \psi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Now we prove the converse.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{b} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:forall-x-phi-and-forall-x-psi-are-satisfied}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \ \text{and}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) be arbitrary.
    By \eqref{eq:forall-x-phi-and-forall-x-psi-are-satisfied} and \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}}} \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}}} \)
    are both true;
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright)}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) holds.
    Since \( e_{1} \) is arbitrary, we can validly infer
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    In other words, \( \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \psi \models \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \).
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-consequence:universally-quantified-disjuncts-implies-universally-quantified-disjunction}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) denotes any \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable, then
    \begin{equation*}
        \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \forall x \, \psi \models \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright]
    \end{equation*}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \theta \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \) and \( \lambda \coloneq \forall x \, \psi \). Furthermore, let \( \phi \coloneq \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \).
    The goal is to prove that \( \theta \,\,{\lor}\,\, \lambda \models \forall x \, \phi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{a} \) denote a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \)
    under \( \mathfrak{A} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \theta \,\,{\lor}\,\, \lambda \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that either \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    or \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    We must show that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is implied in either case.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary.
    Clearly, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds,
    which in turn implies \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( e_{0} \) is arbitrary, we conclude that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Let \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary.
    It follows immediately that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) is true.
    But this would mean that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) also holds.
    Since \( e_{1} \) is arbitrary, we conclude that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    We have proven that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) follows from
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \theta \,\,{\lor}\,\, \lambda \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) in all possible cases.
    Since \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \) are both arbitrary, we can validly conclude \( \theta \,\,{\lor}\,\, \lambda \models \forall x \, \phi \)
    as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:existential-quantifier-distributes-over-disjunction}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is any \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable, then
    \begin{equation*}
        \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \equiv \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure. Let \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is, there exists
    some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright) }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds.
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that either
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:either-phi-or-psi-is-satisfied-with-e0}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \ \text{or}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation}
    If \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \), then
    clearly \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Similarly, if \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \),
    then \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Thus, we infer by \aref{rule-of-inference:constructive-dilemma}{Constructive Dilemma} that either
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) or
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true;
    i.e., \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    Given that \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \) are arbitrary,
    we conclude that \( \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \models \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Now we proceed to establish the converse.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) denote any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure.
    Let \( \mathfrak{b} \) denote any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \) holds;
    that is, either
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:either-exists-x-varphi-or-exists-x-psi}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \ \text{or}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}}
    \end{equation}
    If \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \), then there clearly exists
    an \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \).
    But this implies that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright)}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \)
    and thus \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Similarly, if \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \), then there exists some \( e_{2} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \)
    such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{2}} } \).
    Again, this implies \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright)}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{2}} } \)
    and thus \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    Clearly, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \) follows from
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \) for any structure
    \( \mathfrak{B} \) and any variable assignment function \( \mathfrak{b} \);
    i.e., \( \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \psi \models \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \).
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-consequence:existentially-quantified-conjunction-implies-existentially-quantified-conjuncts}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable, then
    \begin{equation*}
        \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \mright] \models \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \exists x \, \psi
    \end{equation*}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \theta \coloneq \exists x \, \varphi \) and \( \lambda \coloneq \exists x \, \psi \). Let \( \phi \coloneq \varphi \,\,{\land}\,\, \psi \).
    The goal is to prove that \( \exists x \, \phi \models \theta \,\,{\land}\,\, \lambda \). Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure
    and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Furthermore, suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that there exists some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \)
    such that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds.
    This means that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) are both true.
    Therefore, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{\mathfrak{a}} \) and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    are also both true by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:grouping-disjuncts-into-quantified-formula}
    If \( \psi \) and \( \varphi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable such that \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), then
    the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:disjunct-into-all}
        \item \( \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:disjunct-into-exists}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    We begin the proof for \ref{enum:disjunct-into-all} by organizing our notation.
    Let \( \phi \coloneq \forall x \, \varphi \), \( \theta \coloneq \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \), and \( \lambda \coloneq \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \phi \).
    The goal is to show that \( \lambda \equiv \forall x \, \theta \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is, either \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \)
    or \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Let \( e_{0} \) be an arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \).
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \) by assumption, it follows that
    \( \restrictedfunctionsmall{\mathfrak{a}}{\folfreevars{\psi}} = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{\folfreevars{\psi}} \).
    By \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula},
    it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) iff
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}}} \).
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true by assumption,
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) must therefore also be true.
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds.
    Since \( e_{0} \) is arbitrary, we may conclude that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    {\scshape Case:} \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Let \( e_{1} \) be an arbitrary element of \( \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \).
    Thus, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) is true by assumption.
    But this would mean that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) is also true.
    With \( e_{1} \) being arbitrary, it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    We have thus shown that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is implied by
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{a}} \) in all possible scenarios.
    Since \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \) are arbitrary, we can therefore conclude that \( \lambda \models \forall x \, \theta \).
    To prove the converse, we follow a similar procedure as above. Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) denote any \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \)
    denote any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}, it follows that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:satisfaction-of-forall-x-theta}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} } \ \ \text{or}
        \ \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} }
    \end{equation}
    for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    Recall that \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), which means that
    \( \restrictedfunctionsmall{\mathfrak{b}}{ \folfreevars{\psi} } =
       \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} }{ \folfreevars{\psi} }
    \) for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    By \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula},
    it follows that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:satisfaction-of-psi-is-independent-of-x}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{b}} \ \text{iff}
        \ \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} }
    \end{equation}
    for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \).
    By the \aref{rule-of-inference:law-of-excluded-middle}{Law of Excluded Middle}, there are clearly only two possibilities:
    either \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{b}} \) or \( \folwffnonsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    In the case of the former, then it is obvious that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{b}} \) is also true.
    On the other hand, if the latter is true, then \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} } \)
    must be true for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \);
    otherwise, it would contradict \eqref{eq:satisfaction-of-forall-x-theta}.
    But if \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e} } \)
    is true for all \( e \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \), then it must be that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{b}} \), which in turn implies
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{b}} \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Thus, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\lambda}{\mathfrak{b}} \) follows from \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \)
    in all possible scenarios. Since \( \mathfrak{B} \) and \( \mathfrak{b} \) are arbitrary, it follows that \( \forall x \, \theta \models \lambda \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    The proof for \ref{enum:disjunct-into-exists} is a lot more straightforward.
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), it follows from \cref{logical-equivalence:quantification-of-a-formula-with-a-non-free-variable-is-equivalent-to-the-original-formula}
    that \( \psi \equiv \exists x \, \psi \).
    By \cref{logical-equivalence:existential-quantifier-distributes-over-disjunction},
    we also have \( \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright] \equiv \exists x \, \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \varphi \).
    By the \aref{logical-equivalence:substitution-theorem}{Substitution Theorem}, we infer
    \( \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright] \) as required.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:grouping-conjuncts-into-quantified-formula}
    If \( \psi \) and \( \varphi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable such that
    \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \(  \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \mright]  \) \phantomsection \label{enum:conjuncts-into-all}
        \item \(  \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \mright]  \) \phantomsection \label{enum:conjuncts-into-exists}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), we have by \cref{logical-equivalence:quantification-of-a-formula-with-a-non-free-variable-is-equivalent-to-the-original-formula}
    \(  \psi \equiv \forall x \, \psi  \). Furthermore, \cref{logical-equivalence:universal-quantifiers-distributes-over-conjunction} implies that
    \( \forall x \, \mleft[ \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \mright] \equiv \forall x \, \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \forall x \, \varphi \).
    As such, \ref{enum:conjuncts-into-all} follows immediately from the \aref{logical-equivalence:substitution-theorem}{Substitution Theorem}.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    To prove \ref{enum:conjuncts-into-exists}, let \( \phi \coloneq \exists x \, \varphi \) and \( \theta \coloneq \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \).
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{A} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \phi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds.
    Thus, both \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) are true.
    In other words, there exists some \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) holds.
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), it follows that
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{\folfreevars{\psi}} =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{\folfreevars{\psi}}
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{a}} \) holds,
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula} guarantees that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) is also true.
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) and 
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) are both true,
    it follows that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\theta}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \) is true.
    But this also means that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true.
    Since \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \) are arbitrary, it follows that \( \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \phi \models \exists x \, \theta \).

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    Next, we proceed to establish the converse.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be an \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure.
    Let \( \mathfrak{b} \) be a variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under \( \mathfrak{B} \).
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\mleft( \exists x \, \theta \mright)}{\mathfrak{b}} \);
    that is, there exists some \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \)
    and \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) both hold.
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), it follows that,
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{\folfreevars{\psi}} =
        \restrictedfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} }{\folfreevars{\psi}}
    \end{equation*}
    We know that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) is true,
    so \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\psi}{\mathfrak{b}} \) must also be true by
    \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}.
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \) holds, we can further infer
    that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\phi}{\mathfrak{b}} \) is true.
    Taking all these facts into account, we can validly infer that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \phi \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}}\)
    holds. Since \( \mathfrak{B} \) and \( \mathfrak{b} \) are arbitrary, \( \exists x \, \theta \models \psi \,\,{\land}\,\, \phi \) follows readily.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:grouping-antecedent-into-quantified-consequent}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable such that \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \),
    then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \psi {\implies} \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \psi {\implies} \varphi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:universal-consequent}
        \item \( \psi {\implies} \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi {\implies} \varphi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:existential-consequent}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    We begin with the following facts:
    \begin{align*}
        \psi \implies \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \lnot \, \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \forall x \, \varphi \phantomsection\\
        \psi \implies \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \lnot \, \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \exists x \, \varphi \phantomsection
    \end{align*}
    Since \( x \notin \folvars{\psi} \), we have by \cref{logical-equivalence:grouping-disjuncts-into-quantified-formula}
    and the \aref{logical-equivalence:substitution-theorem}{Substitution Theorem}
    \begin{gather*}
        \psi \implies \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \lnot \, \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright]
        \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \psi \implies \varphi \mright]\\
        \psi \implies \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \lnot \, \psi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \varphi \mright]
        \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \psi \implies  \varphi \mright] \qedhere
    \end{gather*}
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:grouping-consequent-into-quantified-antecedent}
    If \( \varphi \) and \( \psi \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-formulas and \( x \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-variable such that \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), then
    the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \forall x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi {\implies} \psi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:universal-antecedent}
        \item \( \exists x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi {\implies} \psi \mright] \) \phantomsection \label{enum:existential-antecedent}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    We begin with the definition of the \( {\implies} \) symbol like so:
    \begin{align*}
        \forall x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \lnot \, \forall x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi\\
        \exists x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \lnot \, \exists x \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi
    \end{align*}
    By \cref{logical-equivalence:negated-quantified-formulas} and the \aref{logical-equivalence:substitution-theorem}{Substitution Theorem}, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \forall x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \exists x \, \lnot \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi\\
        \exists x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \forall x \, \lnot \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi
    \end{align*}
    Since \( x \notin \folfreevars{\psi} \), \cref{logical-equivalence:grouping-disjuncts-into-quantified-formula} applies and the equivalences become
    \begin{align*}
        \forall x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \lnot \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \equiv \exists x \, \mleft[ \varphi {\implies} \psi \mright]\\
        \exists x \, \varphi {\implies} \psi &\equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \lnot \, \varphi \,\,{\lor}\,\, \psi \mright] \equiv \forall x \, \mleft[ \varphi {\implies} \psi \mright]
        \qedhere
    \end{align*}
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \phantomsection \label{logical-equivalence:changing-free-variables-does-not-affect-quantification}
    If \( \varphi \) is any \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula such that \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{\varphi} \)
    for \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables \( x \) and \( y \), then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \forall x \, \varphi \equiv \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \) \phantomsection \label{enum:variable-change-for-universal-quantifier}
        \item \( \exists x \, \varphi \equiv \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \) \phantomsection \label{enum:variable-change-for-existential-quantifier}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \mathfrak{A} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{a} \) be an arbitrary variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under
    \( \mathfrak{A} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \); that is,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:varphi-is-satisfied-by-A-using-a}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e} }
        \ \ \text{for all} \ e \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}}
    \end{equation}
    Let \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) be arbitrary. It follows immediately from \eqref{eq:varphi-is-satisfied-by-A-using-a} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Furthermore, since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), \cref{thm:satisfaction-of-formulas-with-replaced-free-variables} entails
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) } }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) = e_{0} \), we can infer from the previous statement
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{ e_{0} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Note that \(  \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}} = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}\)
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}.
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Writing out the definition of 
    \(  \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } \) in full, we get
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } =
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \mleft( \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{\folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\}} \,{\union}\, \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\} \mright) }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Note that \( \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{\folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\}} \)
    and \( \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\} \) are compatible functions, so we have by
    \cref{thm:metatheory-restriction-of-union-of-compatible-functions-equals-the-union-of-restrictions,thm:metatheory-restriction-of-restricted-function-equals-original-function-restricted-to-intersection}
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } =
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \,{\intersect}\, \mleft( \folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\} \mright) } \,{\union}\, \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    But \( \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \subseteq \folvars{\mathcal{L}} \) and \( x \notin \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \), which further reduces the equality to
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } =
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } \,{\union}\, \emptyset =
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Putting everything together, we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{ e_{0} } } \) is true,
    we infer from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula}
    that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    That is, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    Since \( \mathfrak{A} \) and \( \mathfrak{a} \) are arbitrary, we conclude that \( \forall x \, \varphi \models \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \).
    The converse can be established with a similar argument.
    Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \mleft( \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \mright) }{\mathfrak{a}} \) is true.
    Thus, for any arbitrary \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \),
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}}  }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }\) as previously
    shown, it follows from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    Moreover, \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) = e_{0}\), so the statement is synonymous with
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) } }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), \cref{thm:satisfaction-of-formulas-with-replaced-free-variables} applies and we infer
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{a}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{A}} \) is arbitrary, we conclude that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{A}}{\mleft( \forall x \, \varphi \mright)}{\mathfrak{a}} \).
    In other words, \( \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \models \forall x \, \varphi \).
    The truth of \ref{enum:variable-change-for-universal-quantifier} follows from the fact that \( \forall x \, \varphi \models \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \) and \( \forall y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \models \forall x \, \varphi \) are both true.

    \vspace{0.3cm}
    \noindent
    The argument to prove \ref{enum:variable-change-for-existential-quantifier} is nearly identical.
    Let \( \mathfrak{B} \) be an arbitrary \( \mathcal{L} \)-structure and \( \mathfrak{b} \) be any variable assignment function of \( \mathcal{L} \) under
    \( \mathfrak{B} \). Suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}} \); that is, there exists
    an \( e_{0} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) such that \(  \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \varphi }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} } \).
    Since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), it follows from
    \cref{thm:satisfaction-of-formulas-with-replaced-free-variables} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{b}{x}{e_{0}} } {y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) }}
    \end{equation*}
    But \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}}(x) = e_{0} \), so the statement is synonymous with
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}} }
    \end{equation*}
    Note that \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}} \)
    and \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}} \) are equal
    functions by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct} (because \( x \) and \( y \) are distinct \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables).
    By \cref{dfn:first-order-language-modified-variable-assignment-function},
    \begin{equation*}
        \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}} =
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\} }
        \,\,{\union}\,\, \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\}
    \end{equation*}
    Clearly \( \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\} } \)
    and \( \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\} \) are compatible functions.
    We now apply \cref{thm:metatheory-restriction-of-restricted-function-equals-original-function-restricted-to-intersection,thm:metatheory-restriction-of-union-of-compatible-functions-equals-the-union-of-restrictions} to obtain the restriction of \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}} \)
    on \( \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \):
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \,{\intersect}\, \mleft( \folvars{\mathcal{L}} - \mleft\{ x \mright\} \mright) } \,\,{\union}\,\, \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \mleft\{ \orderedpair{x}{e_{0}} \mright\} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Note that \( x \notin \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \)
    and \( \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } \subseteq \folvars{\mathcal{L}} \).
    Thus,
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{x}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } } \,\,{\union}\,\,
        \emptyset
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{0}} }{y}{e_{0}} } \) is true, we infer from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula} that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{0}} } \)
    is also true. In other words, \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}} \).
    \begin{equation*}
        \therefore \, \exists x \, \varphi \models \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y}
    \end{equation*}
    Conversely, suppose that \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{\mathfrak{b}} \) is true. That is,
    there exists some \( e_{1} \in \abs{\mathfrak{B}} \) such that
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} } \).
    By a similar line of reasoning, we can infer that
    \begin{equation*}
        \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} }{x}{e_{1}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
        = \restrictedfunctionsmall{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} }{ \folfreevars{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} } }
    \end{equation*}
    and subsequently deduce from \cref{thm:first-order-language-different-variable-assignment-functions-yield-the-same-interpretation-if-they-agree-on-the-free-variables-of-a-formula} that
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} }{x}{e_{1}} }
    \end{equation*}
    Note that \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{y}{e_{1}} }{x}{e_{1}}
    = \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} }{y}{e_{1}} \)
    by \cref{thm:first-order-language-the-order-of-variable-modification-of-modified-variable-assignment-functions-does-not-matter-as-long-as-the-variable-symbols-are-distinct}
    since \( x \) and \( y \) are different \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables. Thus,
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} }{y}{e_{1}} }
    \end{equation*}
    must also be true. However, \( \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} = e_{1} \), which means that the previous statement is synonymous with
    \begin{equation*}
        \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} }{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} }{y}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}}(x) } }
    \end{equation*}
    Since \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folfreevars{\varphi} \), we have
    \(  \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{\varphi}{ \folmodifiedvariableassignmentfunction{\mathfrak{b}}{x}{e_{1}} } \)
    by \cref{thm:satisfaction-of-formulas-with-replaced-free-variables} and thus
    \( \folwffsatisfaction{\mathfrak{B}}{ \mleft( \exists x \, \varphi \mright) }{\mathfrak{b}} \) by \cref{dfn:first-order-language-satisfaction-of-formulas}.
    \begin{equation*}
        \therefore \, \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \models \exists x \, \varphi
    \end{equation*}
    The fact that
    \( \exists x \, \varphi \models \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \) and
    \( \exists y \, \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \models \exists x \, \varphi \) are both true suffices to establish
    \ref{enum:variable-change-for-existential-quantifier}.
\end{proof}

\begin{thm}
    \label{logical-equivalence:equivalent-quantified-forms-of-formulas-with-free-variables-replaced}
    If \( \varphi \) is an \( \mathcal{L} \)-formula, and \( x \) and \( y \) are \( \mathcal{L} \)-variables such that
    \( x \in \folfreevars{\varphi} \) and \( y \notin \folvars{\varphi} \), then the following hold:
    \begin{enumerate}[topsep=2pt,label=\textup{\texttt{(\alph*)}}]
        \item \( \forall x \, \mleft[ \mleft( x = y \mright) {\implies} \varphi \mright] \equiv \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \)
            \phantomsection \label{enum:free-variable-replaced-formula-universal-quantifier-equivalent}
        \item \( \exists x \, \mleft[ \mleft( x = y \mright) \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \mright] \equiv \folfreevariablereplacement{\varphi}{x}{y} \)
            \phantomsection \label{enum:free-variable-replaced-formula-existential-quantifier-equivalent}
    \end{enumerate}
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
    Let \( \psi \coloneq \mleft( x = y \mright) {\implies} \varphi \) and \( \phi \coloneq \mleft( x = y \mright) \,\,{\land}\,\, \varphi \).
